I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.228, F41.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s kind of embarrassing, but I’m here because of elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. Maybe it could happen somewhere else if I felt stuck, but elevators are the thing I’m here about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel this same kind of fear in a situation that isn't an elevator, if you felt just as trapped or unable to escape?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I don’t want to say it could never happen. If I felt just as trapped and like I couldn’t get out, I might get that same scared feeling. But really, the thing I keep running into is elevators. I know they’re not actually dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. Maybe it could happen somewhere else if I felt stuck, but elevators are the thing I’m here about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel this same kind of fear in a situation that isn't an elevator, if you felt just as trapped or unable to escape?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I don’t want to say it could never happen. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. Maybe it could happen somewhere else if I felt stuck, but elevators are the thing I’m here about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel this same kind of fear in a situation that isn't an elevator, if you felt just as trapped or unable to escape?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I don’t want to say it could never happen. If I felt just as trapped and like I couldn’t get out, I might get that same scared feeling. But really, the thing I keep running into is elevators. I know they’re not actually dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could you feel this same kind of fear in any other situation where you feel trapped, not just in an elevator?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, if I really felt trapped, it could feel similar. But I don’t want to overstate it. The reason I’m here is mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could your fear of being trapped happen in a situation that has nothing to do with elevators?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could, if I felt really stuck, but I don’t want to make it sound like it’s happening everywhere. What I know for sure is elevators. I know they aren’t really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation, other than being in an elevator, where feeling trapped would cause you the same level of fear?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’ve been trying to think of one, and I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. Elevators are the main thing I actually notice it with. I’m sure nobody likes feeling stuck, but I can’t think of another situation that gives me that same level of fear.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else besides elevators that you avoid because you fear being trapped?\n\nPatient: Mostly just elevators, yeah. I mean… I do take the stairs most days at my apartment and on job sites, but I kind of tell myself that’s just easier anyway. If an elevator or one of those lifts is the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do different work, or I’ll ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. I hope that makes sense—I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could your fear of being trapped happen in a situation that has nothing to do with elevators?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could, if I felt really stuck, but I don’t want to make it sound like it’s happening everywhere. What I know for sure is elevators. I know they aren’t really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation, other than being in an elevator, where feeling trapped would cause you the same level of fear?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’ve been trying to think of one, and I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. Elevators are the main thing I actually notice it with. I’m sure nobody likes feeling stuck, but I can’t think of another situation that gives me that same level of fear.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else besides elevators that you avoid because you fear being trapped?\n\nPatient: Mostly just elevators, yeah. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s kind of embarrassing, but I’m here because of elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s kind of embarrassing, but I’m here because of elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. Maybe it could happen somewhere else if I felt stuck, but elevators are the thing I’m here about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel this same kind of fear in a situation that isn't an elevator, if you felt just as trapped or unable to escape?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I don’t want to say it could never happen. If I felt just as trapped and like I couldn’t get out, I might get that same scared feeling. But really, the thing I keep running into is elevators. I know they’re not actually dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could you feel this same kind of fear in any other situation where you feel trapped, not just in an elevator?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, if I really felt trapped, it could feel similar. But I don’t want to overstate it. The reason I’m here is mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could your fear of being trapped happen in a situation that has nothing to do with elevators?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could, if I felt really stuck, but I don’t want to make it sound like it’s happening everywhere. What I know for sure is elevators. I know they aren’t really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation, other than being in an elevator, where feeling trapped would cause you the same level of fear?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’ve been trying to think of one, and I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. Elevators are the main thing I actually notice it with. I hope that makes sense—I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other situation, besides an elevator, where you feel trapped and it causes you a significant amount of fear?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t think of another one that hits me like that. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s kind of embarrassing, but I’m here because of elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. Maybe it could happen somewhere else if I felt stuck, but elevators are the thing I’m here about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel this same kind of fear in a situation that isn't an elevator, if you felt just as trapped or unable to escape?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I don’t want to say it could never happen. If I felt just as trapped and like I couldn’t get out, I might get that same scared feeling. But really, the thing I keep running into is elevators. I know they’re not actually dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could you feel this same kind of fear in any other situation where you feel trapped, not just in an elevator?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, if I really felt trapped, it could feel similar. But I don’t want to overstate it. The reason I’m here is mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could your fear of being trapped happen in a situation that has nothing to do with elevators?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could, if I felt really stuck, but I don’t want to make it sound like it’s happening everywhere. What I know for sure is elevators. I know they aren’t really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation, other than being in an elevator, where feeling trapped would cause you the same level of fear?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’ve been trying to think of one, and I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. Elevators are the main thing I actually notice it with. I’m sure nobody likes feeling stuck, but I can’t think of another situation that gives me that same level of fear.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else besides elevators that you avoid because you fear being trapped?\n\nPatient: Mostly just elevators, yeah. I mean… I do take the stairs most days at my apartment and on job sites, but I kind of tell myself that’s just easier anyway. If an elevator or one of those lifts is the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do different work, or I’ll ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. I hope that makes sense—I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other situation, besides an elevator, where you feel trapped and it causes you a significant amount of fear?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t think of another one that hits me like that. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s kind of embarrassing, but I’m here because of elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, and most people use them all the time, but when I have to get in one I get really scared, like I’m going to get trapped in there. I try to tell myself it’s fine, but I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience similar feelings of fear or anxiety in situations other than being in elevators?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think so. I mean, everybody gets nervous about certain things, right? But this elevator thing is the one I notice. I haven’t really had that same kind of fear anywhere else, at least not that I can think of.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding elevators because of this fear?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, but I try not to make a big deal out of it. Most days I’ll just take the stairs at my apartment or on job sites. If the elevator or lift is really the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do something else… or I can ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode where you felt an intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes, with symptoms like a racing heart, sweating, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it an “episode,” exactly, but when the elevator doors start sliding shut, it comes on pretty fast. My chest gets tight, my hands get sweaty, and all I can think is, I need to get out. Even if it’s only a few floors. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about having another episode of intense fear like the ones you have in elevators in other situations?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t sit around worrying it’ll happen just anywhere. It’s more like if I know there’s gonna be an elevator involved, then I start thinking about it. Other situations, not really… at least I haven’t noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of elevators specifically about being in a small, enclosed space, or is it more about something else, like the elevator breaking down or something happening while you're in it?\n\nPatient: It’s more the trapped part, I think. Like, I know in my head elevators usually aren’t dangerous and people ride them all day, but once I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be stuck in there and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. It sounds kind of silly when I say it, but… yeah, that’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling anxious or fearful in any other enclosed spaces, like tunnels or small rooms?\n\nPatient: Not really that I’ve noticed… I mean, small rooms don’t usually bother me unless I start thinking I can’t get out, I guess. It’s mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I just get this fear like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I can’t make myself feel normal about it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear only about being trapped in an elevator, or would you feel similarly scared if you were in another situation where you felt trapped, like in a crowded room with no clear exit?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the elevator… but I guess it’s the trapped feeling underneath it, if that makes sense. Like, I know elevators aren’t really dangerous, and I feel kind of embarrassed saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared like I’m going to be stuck in there, and I can’t calm myself down the way I should. A crowded room… I don’t know, maybe if I really felt like I couldn’t get out, but I don’t usually think of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your fear of being trapped is limited to elevators or could it potentially apply to other situations as well?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mainly elevators, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. It’s just… I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get this fear that I’ll be trapped in there, and I can’t really talk myself out of it. Maybe it could happen somewhere else if I felt stuck, but elevators are the thing I’m here about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever feel this same kind of fear in a situation that isn't an elevator, if you felt just as trapped or unable to escape?\n\nPatient: Maybe… I mean, I don’t want to say it could never happen. If I felt just as trapped and like I couldn’t get out, I might get that same scared feeling. But really, the thing I keep running into is elevators. I know they’re not actually dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could you feel this same kind of fear in any other situation where you feel trapped, not just in an elevator?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, if I really felt trapped, it could feel similar. But I don’t want to overstate it. The reason I’m here is mostly elevators. I know they’re not really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Could your fear of being trapped happen in a situation that has nothing to do with elevators?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess it could, if I felt really stuck, but I don’t want to make it sound like it’s happening everywhere. What I know for sure is elevators. I know they aren’t really dangerous, but when I have to get in one, I get scared like I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t make myself feel normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation, other than being in an elevator, where feeling trapped would cause you the same level of fear?\n\nPatient: Not really… I’ve been trying to think of one, and I don’t want to give you the wrong answer just to be helpful. Elevators are the main thing I actually notice it with. I’m sure nobody likes feeling stuck, but I can’t think of another situation that gives me that same level of fear.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else besides elevators that you avoid because you fear being trapped?\n\nPatient: Mostly just elevators, yeah. I mean… I do take the stairs most days at my apartment and on job sites, but I kind of tell myself that’s just easier anyway. If an elevator or one of those lifts is the only way up, I’ll usually ask if I can do different work, or I’ll ride it if someone I trust is right there with me. I hope that makes sense—I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any other situation, besides an elevator, where you feel trapped and it causes you a significant amount of fear?\n\nPatient: I honestly can’t think of another one that hits me like that. It’s really the elevator thing. Like, I know it’s not actually dangerous, and I feel a little silly saying it, but when I have to get in one I get scared I’m going to be trapped in there, and I just can’t talk myself into feeling normal about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of being trapped would cause you significant distress in a situation where you couldn't escape, even if it had nothing to do with an elevator?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure, honestly. I mean, I guess if I really couldn’t get out of somewhere, anybody would get upset, right? But I haven’t noticed that same kind of panic with other places the way I do with elevators. I don’t want to say yes if I’m just guessing.\n\nDoctor: Is there any situation, other than being in an elevator, where you feel like you cannot escape and it causes you fear?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’m trying to think of something, but nothing really comes to mind besides elevators. If that’s okay to say… it’s just that one situation that seems to set me off.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of being trapped something that you think could be triggered by a situation where you feel you cannot escape, regardless of whether it involves an elevator or not?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe it could, but I only really know it with elevators.
